militia of students (*talib*) from Islamic [madrassas (schools) in Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrassas_in_Pakistan "Madrassas in Pakistan"),[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch-1998-171)[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-173) who soon had military support from Pakistan.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-George_Washington_University-174) Taking control of [Kandahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandahar "Kandahar") city that year,[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch-1998-171) they conquered more territories until finally driving out the government of [Rabbani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burhanuddin_Rabbani "Burhanuddin Rabbani") from Kabul in 1996,[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175)[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176) where they established an [emirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan_\(1996%E2%80%932001\) "Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan (1996–2001)").[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177) The Taliban were condemned internationally for the harsh enforcement of their interpretation of Islamic [sharia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharia "Sharia") law, which resulted in the brutal treatment of many Afghans, especially [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban_treatment_of_women "Taliban treatment of women").[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178)[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-179) During their rule, the Taliban and their allies committed massacres against Afghan civilians, denied UN food supplies to starving civilians and conducted a policy of [scorched earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorched_earth "Scorched earth"), burning vast areas of fertile land and destroying tens of thousands of homes.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181)[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182)[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183)[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184)[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185)\[*[excessive citations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#Bundling_citations "Wikipedia:Citing sources")*]

[After the fall of Kabul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kabul_\(1992%E2%80%931996\) "Battle of Kabul (1992–1996)") to the Taliban, [Ahmad Shah Massoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Shah_Massoud "Ahmad Shah Massoud") and [Abdul Rashid Dostum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Rashid_Dostum "Abdul Rashid Dostum") formed the [Northern Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Alliance "Northern Alliance"), later joined by others, to resist the Taliban. Dostum's forces were defeated by the Taliban during the [Battles of Mazar-i-Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battles_of_Mazar-i-Sharif_\(1997%E2%80%931998\) "Battles of Mazar-i-Sharif (1997–1998)") in 1997 and 1998; Pakistan's Chief of Army Staff, [Pervez Musharraf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf "Pervez Musharraf"), began sending thousands of Pakistanis to help the Taliban defeat the Northern Alliance.[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-186)[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-George_Washington_University-174)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-188)[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-189)\[*[excessive citations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#Bundling_citations "Wikipedia:Citing sources")*] By 2000, the Northern Alliance only controlled 10% of territory, cornered in the northeast. On 9 September 2001, Massoud was assassinated by two Arab [suicide attackers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_attack "Suicide attack") in [Panjshir Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panjshir_Valley "Panjshir Valley"). Around 400,000 Afghans died in internal conflicts between 1990 and 2001.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-190)

### US invasion and Islamic Republic

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: US invasion and Islamic Republic")]

Further information: [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%932021\) "War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)"), [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan "Islamic Republic of Afghanistan"), [Taliban insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban_insurgency "Taliban insurgency"), and [Fall of Kabul (2021)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Kabul_\(2021\) "Fall of Kabul (2021)")

In October 2001, the [United States invaded Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan "United States invasion of Afghanistan") to remove the Taliban from power after they refused to hand over [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden "Osama bin Laden"), the prime suspect of the [September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks"), who was a "guest" of the Taliban and was operating his [al-Qaeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda "Al-Qaeda") network in Afghanistan.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191)[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192)[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-193) The majority of Afghans supported the American invasion.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-194)[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195) During the initial invasion, US and UK forces bombed al-Qaeda training camps, and later working with the Northern Alliance, the Taliban regime came to an end.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Inbound_Choppers_in_Afghanistan_2008.jpg/220px-Inbound_Choppers_in_Afghanistan_2008.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Inbound_Choppers_in_Afghanistan_2008.jpg)

US troops and [Chinooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_CH-47_Chinook "Boeing CH-47 Chinook") in Afghanistan, 2008

In December 2001, after the Taliban government was overthrown, the [Afghan Interim Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_Interim_Administration "Afghan Interim Administration") under [Hamid Karzai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamid_Karzai "Hamid Karzai") was formed. The [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force "International Security Assistance Force") (ISAF) was established by the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council "UN Security Council") to help assist the [Karzai administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karzai_administration "Karzai administration") and provide basic security.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197)[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-198) By this time, after two decades of war as well as an acute [famine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famine "Famine") at the time, Afghanistan had one of the highest [infant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality "Infant mortality") and child mortality rates in the world, the lowest life expectancy, much of the population were hungry,[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-199)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-200)[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-201) and infrastructure was in ruins.[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-202) Many foreign donors started providing aid and assistance to rebuild the war-torn country.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203)[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204) As coalition troops entered Afghanistan to help the [rebuilding process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_in_Afghanistan "Reconstruction in Afghanistan"),[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205)[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) the [Taliban began an insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban_insurgency "Taliban insurgency") to regain control. Afghanistan remained one of the poorest countries in the world because of a lack of foreign investment, [government corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_in_Afghanistan "Corruption in Afghanistan"), and the Taliban insurgency.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207)[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208)

The Afghan government was able to build some democratic structures, adopting a constitution in 2004 with the name [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan "Islamic Republic of Afghanistan"). Attempts were made, often with the support of foreign donor countries, to improve the country's economy, healthcare, education, transport, and agriculture. ISAF forces also began to train the [Afghan National Security Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_National_Security_Forces "Afghan National Security Forces"). Following 2002, nearly five million [Afghans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_diaspora "Afghan diaspora") were repatriated.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-209) The number of NATO troops present in Afghanistan peaked at 140,000 in 2011,[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-210) dropping to about 16,000 in 2018.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-211) In September 2014 [Ashraf Ghani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashraf_Ghani "Ashraf Ghani") became president after the [2014 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_presidential_election,_2014 "Afghan presidential election, 2014") where for the first time in Afghanistan's history power was democratically transferred.[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-212)[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-213)[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-214) On 28 December 2014, NATO formally ended ISAF combat operations and transferred full security responsibility to the Afghan government. The NATO-led [Operation Resolute Support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Resolute_Support "Operation Resolute Support") was formed the same day as a successor to ISAF.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-215)[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-216) Thousands of NATO troops remained in the country to train and advise Afghan government forces[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-217) and continue their fight against the Taliban.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-218) A report titled *Body Count* concluded that 106,000–170,000 civilians had been killed as a result of the fighting in Afghanistan at the hands of all parties to the conflict.[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-219)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/2021_Taliban_Offensive.png/220px-2021_Taliban_Offensive.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2021_Taliban_Offensive.png)

A map of Afghanistan showing the [2021 Taliban offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive "2021 Taliban offensive")

On 19 February 2020, the [US–Taliban deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US%E2%80%93Taliban_deal "US–Taliban deal") was made in Qatar. The deal was one of the critical events that caused the collapse of the [Afghan National Security Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_National_Security_Forces "Afghan National Security Forces") (ANSF);[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-220) following the signing of the deal, the US dramatically reduced the number of air attacks and deprived the ANSF of a critical edge in fighting the [Taliban insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban_insurgency "Taliban insurgency"), leading to the Taliban takeover of Kabul.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-221)

### Second Taliban era

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Second Taliban era")]

Further information: [Aftermath of the Afghanistan War (2001–2021)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftermath_of_the_Afghanistan_War_\(2001%E2%80%932021\) "Aftermath of the Afghanistan War (2001–2021)")

NATO Secretary General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg "Jens Stoltenberg") announced on 14 April 2021 that the alliance had agreed to start [withdrawing its troops from Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%932021_US_troop_withdrawal_from_Afghanistan "2020–2021 US troop withdrawal from Afghanistan") by 1 May.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-222) Soon after NATO troops began withdrawing, the [Taliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban "Taliban") launched an [offensive against the Afghan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive "2021 Taliban offensive") and quickly advanced in front of collapsing Afghan government forces.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-223)[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-224) The Taliban [captured the capital city of Kabul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Kabul_\(2021\) "Fall of Kabul (2021)") on 15 August 2021, after regaining control over a vast majority of Afghanistan. Several foreign diplomats and Afghan government officials, including president Ashraf Ghani,[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-225) were evacuated from the country, with many Afghan civilians attempting to flee along with them.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-226) On 17 August, first vice president [Amrullah Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amrullah_Saleh "Amrullah Saleh") proclaimed himself caretaker president and announced the formation of an [anti-Taliban front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Resistance_Front_of_Afghanistan "National Resistance Front of Afghanistan") with a reported 6,000+ troops[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-227)[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-228) in the [Panjshir Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panjshir_Valley "Panjshir Valley"), along with [Ahmad Massoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Massoud "Ahmad Massoud").[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-229)[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-230) However, by 6 September, the Taliban had taken control of most of [Panjshir province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panjshir_province "Panjshir province"), with resistance fighters retreating to the mountains.[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-231) Clashes in the valley ceased mid-September.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-232)

According to the [Costs of War Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costs_of_War_Project "Costs of War Project"), 176,000 people were killed in the conflict, including 46,319 civilians, between 2001 and 2021.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-233) According to the [Uppsala Conflict Data Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uppsala_Conflict_Data_Program "Uppsala Conflict Data Program"), at least 212,191 people were killed in the conflict.[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-234) Though the state of war in the country ended in 2021, armed conflict persists in some regions[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-235)[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-236)[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-237) amid [fighting between the Taliban and the local branch of the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State%E2%80%93Taliban_conflict "Islamic State–Taliban conflict"), as well as an anti-Taliban [Republican insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_insurgency_in_Afghanistan "Republican insurgency in Afghanistan").[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-238)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Taliban_Humvee_in_Kabul%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png/220px-Taliban_Humvee_in_Kabul%2C_August_2021_%28cropped%29.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taliban_Humvee_in_Kabul,_August_2021_\(cropped\).png)

Taliban fighters in Kabul on a captured [Humvee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humvee "Humvee") following the [2021 fall of Kabul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Kabul_\(2021\) "Fall of Kabul (2021)")

The Taliban government is led by [supreme leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_the_Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan "Head of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan") [Hibatullah Akhundzada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hibatullah_Akhundzada "Hibatullah Akhundzada")[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-239) and acting prime minister [Hasan Akhund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasan_Akhund "Hasan Akhund"), who took office on 7 September 2021.[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-240)[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-241) Akhund is one of the four founders of the Taliban[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-242) and was a deputy prime minister of the previous emirate; his appointment was seen as a compromise between moderates and hardliners.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-243) A [new, all-male cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Afghanistan "Cabinet of Afghanistan") was formed, which included [Abdul Hakim Haqqani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hakim_Haqqani "Abdul Hakim Haqqani") as minister of justice.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-244)[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-245) On 20 September 2021, [United Nations Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary-General_of_the_United_Nations "Secretary-General of the United Nations") [António Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B3nio_Guterres "António Guterres") received a letter from acting [minister of foreign affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Afghanistan\) "Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Afghanistan)") [Amir Khan Muttaqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Khan_Muttaqi "Amir Khan Muttaqi") to formally claim Afghanistan's seat as a member state for their official spokesman in [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha "Doha"), [Suhail Shaheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suhail_Shaheen "Suhail Shaheen"). The United Nations did not recognize the previous Taliban government and chose to work with the then government-in-exile instead.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-246)

Western nations suspended most of their humanitarian aid to Afghanistan following the Taliban's August 2021 takeover of the country; the [World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank "World Bank") and [International Monetary Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund "International Monetary Fund") also halted their payments.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-247)[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-248) More than half of Afghanistan's 39 million people faced an acute [food shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_security "Food security") in October 2021.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-249) Human Rights Watch reported on 11 November 2021 that Afghanistan was facing widespread [famine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famine "Famine") due to an economic and banking crisis.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-250)

The [Taliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban "Taliban") have significantly tackled corruption, improving on the [corruption perceptions index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Perceptions_Index "Corruption Perceptions Index") from 174th to 150th best out of 180 countries from 2021 to 2022,[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-251) but dropping to 162th in 2023.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-252) The Taliban have also reportedly reduced bribery and extortion in public service areas.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-251)

At the same time, the human rights situation in the country has deteriorated.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-253) Following the 2001 invasion, more than 5.7 million refugees returned to Afghanistan;[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-254) however, in 2021, 2.6 million Afghans remained refugees, primarily in Iran and Pakistan, and another 4 million were internally displaced.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-255)

In October 2023, the Pakistani government ordered the [expulsion of Afghans from Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_of_Afghans_from_Pakistan "Expulsion of Afghans from Pakistan").[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-256) Iran also decided to deport [Afghan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghans_in_Iran "Afghans in Iran") nationals back to Afghanistan.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-257) Taliban authorities condemned the deportations of Afghans as an "inhuman act".[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-258) Afghanistan faced a humanitarian crisis in late 2023.[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-259)

On 10 November 2024, Afghanistan's Foreign Ministry confirmed that Taliban representatives would attend the [2024 United Nations Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference "2024 United Nations Climate Change Conference"), marking the first time the country participated since the Taliban's return to power in 2021. Afghanistan had been barred from previous summits due to the lack of global recognition of the Taliban regime. However, the Taliban's environmental officials stressed that climate change is a humanitarian issue, not a political one, and should be addressed regardless of political differences.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-260)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Geography")]

Main article: [Geography of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Afghanistan "Geography of Afghanistan")

*[Noshaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noshaq "Noshaq")*![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/BlackMountain.svg/14px-BlackMountain.svg.png)

Afghanistan is located in Southern-Central Asia.[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-261)[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-262)[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-263)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-264)[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-265) The region centered at Afghanistan is considered the "crossroads of Asia",[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-266) and the country has had the nickname Heart of Asia.[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-267) The renowned [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu "Urdu") poet [Allama Iqbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allama_Iqbal "Allama Iqbal") once wrote about the country:

> Asia is a body of water and earth, of which the Afghan nation is the heart. From its discord, the discord of Asia; and from its accord, the accord of Asia.

At over 652,864 km2 (252,072 sq mi),[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-268) Afghanistan is the world's [41st largest country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area "List of countries and dependencies by area").[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-269) It is slightly bigger than France and smaller than Myanmar, and about the size of Texas in the United States. There is no coastline, as Afghanistan is [landlocked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landlocked "Landlocked"). Afghanistan shares its longest land border (the [Durand Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durand_Line "Durand Line")) with Pakistan to the east and south, followed by borders with Tajikistan to the northeast, Iran to the west, Turkmenistan to the north-west, Uzbekistan to the north and China to the far northeast; India recognizes a border with Afghanistan through Pakistani-administered [Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir "Kashmir").[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-270) Clockwise from south-west, Afghanistan shares borders with the [Sistan and Baluchestan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistan_and_Baluchestan_Province "Sistan and Baluchestan Province"), South Khorasan Province and [Razavi Khorasan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razavi_Khorasan_Province "Razavi Khorasan Province") of Iran; [Ahal Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahal_Region "Ahal Region"), [Mary Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Region "Mary Region") and [Lebap Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebap_Region "Lebap Region") of Turkmenistan; [Surxondaryo Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surxondaryo_Region "Surxondaryo Region") of Uzbekistan; [Khatlon Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khatlon_Region "Khatlon Region") and [Gorno-Badakhshan Autonomous Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorno-Badakhshan_Autonomous_Region "Gorno-Badakhshan Autonomous Region") of Tajikistan; [Xinjiang Uyghur Autonomous Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang "Xinjiang") of China; and the [Gilgit-Baltistan territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilgit-Baltistan "Gilgit-Baltistan"), [Khyber Pakhtunkhwa province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khyber_Pakhtunkhwa "Khyber Pakhtunkhwa") and [Balochistan province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balochistan,_Pakistan "Balochistan, Pakistan") of Pakistan.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-271)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/FrontLines_Environment_Photo_Contest_Winner_-5_%285808476109%29.jpg/220px-FrontLines_Environment_Photo_Contest_Winner_-5_%285808476109%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:FrontLines_Environment_Photo_Contest_Winner_-5_\(5808476109\).jpg)

Floodplain cultivation in the [Wakhan Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_Corridor "Wakhan Corridor"), [Pamir Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamir_Mountains "Pamir Mountains")

The geography in Afghanistan is varied, but is mostly mountainous and rugged, with some unusual mountain ridges accompanied by plateaus and river basins.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Fisher-2002-272) It is dominated by the [Hindu Kush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Kush "Hindu Kush") range, the western extension of the [Himalayas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himalayas "Himalayas") that stretches to eastern [Tibet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibet "Tibet") via the [Pamir Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamir_Mountains "Pamir Mountains") and [Karakoram Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karakoram_Mountains "Karakoram Mountains") in Afghanistan's far north-east. Most of the highest points are in the east consisting of fertile mountain valleys, often considered part of the "[Roof of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roof_of_the_World "Roof of the World")". The Hindu Kush ends at the west-central highlands, creating plains in the north and southwest, namely the [Turkestan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkestan "Turkestan") Plains and the [Sistan Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistan_Basin "Sistan Basin"); these two regions consist of rolling grasslands and semi-deserts, and hot windy deserts, respectively.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-273) Forests exist in the corridor between [Nuristan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuristan "Nuristan") and [Paktika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paktika "Paktika") provinces (see [East Afghan montane conifer forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Afghan_montane_conifer_forests "East Afghan montane conifer forests")),[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-274) and [tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tundra "Tundra") in the northeast. The country's highest point is [Noshaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noshaq "Noshaq"), at 7,492 m (24,580 ft) above sea level.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Factbook-275) The lowest point lies in [Jowzjan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jowzjan_Province "Jowzjan Province") along the Amu River bank, at 258 m (846 ft) above sea level.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Afghan_topo_en.jpg/330px-Afghan_topo_en.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Afghan_topo_en.jpg)

The mountainous [topography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topography "Topography") of Afghanistan

Despite having numerous rivers and [reservoirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dams_and_reservoirs_in_Afghanistan "List of dams and reservoirs in Afghanistan"), large parts of the country are dry. The [endorheic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endorheic "Endorheic") Sistan Basin is one of the driest regions in the world.[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-276) The [Amu Darya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amu_Darya "Amu Darya") rises at the north of the Hindu Kush, while the nearby [Hari Rud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hari_Rud "Hari Rud") flows west towards [Herat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herat "Herat"), and the [Arghandab River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arghandab_River "Arghandab River") from the central region southwards. To the south and west of the Hindu Kush flow a number of streams that are tributaries of the [Indus River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_River "Indus River"),[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Fisher-2002-272) such as the [Helmand River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmand_River "Helmand River"). The [Kabul River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabul_River "Kabul River") flows in an easterly direction to the Indus ending at the Indian Ocean.[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-afghanistans.com-1-277) Afghanistan receives heavy snow during the winter in the [Hindu Kush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Kush "Hindu Kush") and [Pamir Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamir_Mountains "Pamir Mountains"), and the melting snow in the spring season enters the [rivers, lakes, and streams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_Afghanistan "List of rivers of Afghanistan").[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-278)[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-279) However, two-thirds of the country's water flows into the neighboring countries of [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran"), Pakistan, and [Turkmenistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkmenistan "Turkmenistan"). As reported in 2010, the state needs more than US$2 billion to rehabilitate its irrigation systems so that the water is properly managed.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-280)

In Afghanistan, [forest cover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_cover "Forest cover") is around 2% of the total land area, equivalent to 1,208,440 hectares (ha) of forest in 2020, which was unchanged from 1990. In 2020, naturally regenerating forest covered 1,208,440 hectares (ha). Of the naturally regenerating forest, 0% was reported to be [primary forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_forest "Primary forest") (consisting of native tree species with no clearly visible indications of human activity) and around 0% of the forest area was found within protected areas. For the year 2015, 100% of the forest area was reported to be under [public ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_ownership "State ownership").[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-281)[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-282)

The northeastern Hindu Kush [mountain range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_range "Mountain range"), in and around the [Badakhshan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badakhshan_Province "Badakhshan Province") of Afghanistan, is in a [geologically active](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_environment#Geological_activity "Natural environment") area where earthquakes may occur almost every year.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-283) They can be deadly and destructive, causing [landslides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landslide "Landslide") in some parts or [avalanches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Afghan_avalanches "2009 Afghan avalanches") during the winter.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-284) In June 2022, a [destructive 5.9 earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2022_Afghanistan_earthquake "June 2022 Afghanistan earthquake") struck near the border with Pakistan, killing at least 1,150 people and sparking fears of a major humanitarian crisis.[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-285) On 7 October 2023, a [6.3 magnitude earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_Afghanistan_earthquake "October 2023 Afghanistan earthquake") struck northwest of Herat, killing over 1,400 people.[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-286)

### Climate

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Climate")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Koppen-Geiger_Map_AFG_present.svg/290px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_AFG_present.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_AFG_present.svg)

[Köppen climate map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification") of Afghanistan[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-287)

Afghanistan has a [continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_climate "Continental climate") with harsh winters in the [central highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hazarajat "Hazarajat"), the glaciated northeast (around [Nuristan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuristan "Nuristan")), and the [Wakhan Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_Corridor "Wakhan Corridor"), where the average temperature in January is below −15 °C (5 °F) and can reach −26 °C (−15 °F),[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Fisher-2002-272) and hot summers in the low-lying areas of the [Sistan Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistan_Basin "Sistan Basin") of the southwest, the [Jalalabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalalabad "Jalalabad") basin in the east, and the [Turkestan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_Turkestan "Afghan Turkestan") plains along the [Amu River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amu_River "Amu River") in the north, where temperatures average over 35 °C (95 °F) in July[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Factbook-275)[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-288) and can go over 43 °C (109 °F).[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Fisher-2002-272) The country is generally [arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arid "Arid") in the summers, with most rainfall falling between December and April. The lower areas of northern and western Afghanistan are the driest, with precipitation more common in the east. Although proximate to India, Afghanistan is mostly outside the [monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsoon "Monsoon") zone,[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Fisher-2002-272) except the [Nuristan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuristan_Province "Nuristan Province") which occasionally receives summer monsoon rain.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-289)

Although Afghanistan has contributed minimally to global [greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions "Greenhouse gas emissions"), it is one of the most vulnerable countries to [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") and least prepared to cope with its impacts.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-290) [Climate change in Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Afghanistan "Climate change in Afghanistan") is causing more frequent and severe droughts.[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-291)[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-292) Severe drought conditions affect 25 of the country's 34 provinces, impacting over half the population.[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:4-293) These droughts cause [desertification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desertification "Desertification"),[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:43-294)[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-295) reduce [food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_security "Food security")[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-296) and [water security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_security "Water security"),[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-297) disrupt agriculture and cause [internal displacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internally_displaced_person "Internally displaced person").[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-298) Extreme rainfall over short periods is also more likely, increasing the risk of [floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flood "Flood") and [landslides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landslide "Landslide").[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-299) Due to rising temperatures, almost 14% of Afghanistan's glacier coverage was lost between 1990 and 2015[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-300) increasing the risk of [glacial lake outburst floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glacial_lake_outburst_flood "Glacial lake outburst flood").[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-301) By 2050, climate change could displace an additional 5 million people within Afghanistan.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-302)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:2-303)

### Biodiversity

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Biodiversity")]

Main article: [Wildlife of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Afghanistan "Wildlife of Afghanistan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Schneeleopard-_P1020498.jpg/220px-Schneeleopard-_P1020498.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Schneeleopard-_P1020498.jpg)

The [snow leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_leopard "Snow leopard") is the official national animal of Afghanistan.

Several types of mammals exist throughout Afghanistan. [Snow leopards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_leopard "Snow leopard"), [Siberian tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siberian_tiger "Siberian tiger") and [brown bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_bear "Brown bear") live in the high elevation [alpine tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_tundra "Alpine tundra") regions. The [Marco Polo sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo_sheep "Marco Polo sheep") exclusively live in the [Wakhan Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_Corridor "Wakhan Corridor") region of north-east Afghanistan. Foxes, [wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolves "Wolves"), [otters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otter "Otter"), [deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deer "Deer"), [wild sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_sheep "Wild sheep"), [lynx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynx "Lynx") and other big cats populate the mountain forest region of the east. In the semi-desert northern plains, wildlife include a variety of birds, [hedgehogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hedgehog "Hedgehog"), [gophers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopher "Gopher"), and large carnivores such as [jackals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_jackal "Golden jackal") and [hyenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striped_hyena "Striped hyena").[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gritzner-2009-304)

[Gazelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazelle "Gazelle"), [wild pigs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_boar "Wild boar") and jackals populate the [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe "Steppe") plains of the south and west, while [mongoose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongoose "Mongoose") and cheetahs exist in the semi-desert south.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gritzner-2009-304) [Marmots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marmot "Marmot") and [ibex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibex "Ibex") also live in the high mountains of Afghanistan, and [pheasants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pheasant "Pheasant") exist in some parts of the country.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-305) The [Afghan hound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_hound "Afghan hound") is a native breed of dog known for its fast speed and its long hair; it is relatively known in the west.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Wahab-2007-306)

[Endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic "Endemic") fauna of Afghanistan includes the [Afghan flying squirrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_flying_squirrel "Afghan flying squirrel"), [Afghan snowfinch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_snowfinch "Afghan snowfinch"), *[Paradactylodon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradactylodon "Paradactylodon")* (or the "[Paghman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paghman "Paghman") mountain salamander"), *[Stigmella kasyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stigmella_kasyi "Stigmella kasyi")*, *[Vulcaniella kabulensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulcaniella_kabulensis "Vulcaniella kabulensis")*, [Afghan leopard gecko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_leopard_gecko "Afghan leopard gecko"), *[Wheeleria parviflorellus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeleria_parviflorellus "Wheeleria parviflorellus")*, among others. Endemic flora include *[Iris afghanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iris_afghanica "Iris afghanica")*. Afghanistan has a wide variety of birds despite its relatively arid climate – an estimated 460 species of which 235 breed within.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Wahab-2007-306)

The forest region of Afghanistan has vegetation such as [pine trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pine_tree "Pine tree"), [spruce trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spruce_tree "Spruce tree"), [fir trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fir_tree "Fir tree") and [larches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larch "Larch"), whereas the steppe grassland regions consist of [broadleaf trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadleaf_tree "Broadleaf tree"), short grass, [perennial plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perennial_plant "Perennial plant") and [shrublands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrubland "Shrubland"). The colder high elevation regions are composed of hardy grasses and small flowering plants.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gritzner-2009-304) Several regions are designated [protected areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protected_areas_of_Afghanistan "List of protected areas of Afghanistan"); there are three [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks "National parks"): [Band-e Amir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band-e_Amir "Band-e Amir"), [Wakhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wakhan_National_Park "Wakhan National Park") and [Nuristan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuristan_National_Park "Nuristan National Park"). Afghanistan had a 2018 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index "Forest Landscape Integrity Index") mean score of 8.85/10, ranking it 15th globally out of 172 countries.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-307)

## Government and politics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Government and politics")]

Main article: [Politics of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Afghanistan "Politics of Afghanistan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/200229-D-AP390-1529_%2849603221753%29.jpg/220px-200229-D-AP390-1529_%2849603221753%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:200229-D-AP390-1529_\(49603221753\).jpg)

[The Arg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arg,_Kabul "Arg, Kabul") (the Presidential palace) in Kabul, photographed in 2020

Following the effective collapse of the [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan "Islamic Republic of Afghanistan") during the [2021 Taliban offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive "2021 Taliban offensive"), the Taliban declared the country an Islamic Emirate. A new caretaker government was announced on 7 September.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-BBCHardliners-308) As of June 2024[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit), no other country has formally [recognized the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_the_Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan "Recognition of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan") as the *[de jure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_jure "De jure")* government of Afghanistan.[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-309) According to the [V-Dem Democracy indices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-Dem_Democracy_indices "V-Dem Democracy indices") Afghanistan in 2023 was the third least [electoral democratic country in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_in_Asia "Democracy in Asia").[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-310)

A traditional instrument of governance in Afghanistan is the *[loya jirga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loya_jirga "Loya jirga")* (grand assembly), a [Pashtun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pashtun "Pashtun") consultative meeting that was mainly organized for choosing a new [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state "Head of state"), adopting a new constitution, or to settle national or regional issue such as war.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-311) Loya jirgas have been held since at least 1747,[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBarfield2012295-312) with the most recent one occurring in August 2020.[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-313)[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-314)

### Development of Taliban government

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Development of Taliban government")]

Main article: [Government of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan "Government of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan")

See also: [Cabinet of Afghanistan § Islamic Emirate (2021–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Afghanistan#Islamic_Emirate_\(2021%E2%80%93present\) "Cabinet of Afghanistan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Hasan_Akhund.png/127px-Hasan_Akhund.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hasan_Akhund.png)

[Hasan Akhund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasan_Akhund "Hasan Akhund")\
Acting [Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Afghanistan "Prime Minister of Afghanistan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Sirajuddin_Haqqani_%28cropped%29.png/127px-Sirajuddin_Haqqani_%28cropped%29.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sirajuddin_Haqqani_\(cropped\).png)

[Sirajuddin Haqqani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirajuddin_Haqqani "Sirajuddin Haqqani")\
First [Deputy Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Leader_of_the_Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan "Deputy Leader of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan") and Acting [Interior Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interior_Minister_of_Afghanistan "Interior Minister of Afghanistan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Mullah_Yaqoob_in_January_2022.png/127px-Mullah_Yaqoob_in_January_2022.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mullah_Yaqoob_in_January_2022.png)

[Mullah Yaqoob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah_Yaqoob "Mullah Yaqoob")\
Second Deputy Leader and Acting [Defense Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_\(Afghanistan\) "Ministry of Defense (Afghanistan)")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Abdul_Ghani_Baradar.jpg/127px-Abdul_Ghani_Baradar.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abdul_Ghani_Baradar.jpg)

[Abdul Ghani Baradar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Ghani_Baradar "Abdul Ghani Baradar")\
Third Deputy Leader and Acting [First Deputy Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Afghanistan "Prime Minister of Afghanistan")

On 17 August 2021, the leader of the Taliban-affiliated [Hezb-e-Islami Gulbuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezb-e_Islami_Gulbuddin "Hezb-e Islami Gulbuddin") party, [Gulbuddin Hekmatyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulbuddin_Hekmatyar "Gulbuddin Hekmatyar"), met with both [Hamid Karzai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamid_Karzai "Hamid Karzai"), the former [President of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Afghanistan "President of Afghanistan"), and [Abdullah Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_Abdullah "Abdullah Abdullah"), the former chairman of the High Council for National Reconciliation and former [Chief Executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Executive_\(Afghanistan\) "Chief Executive (Afghanistan)"), in [Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha "Doha"), [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar "Qatar"), with the aim of forming a [national unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_unity_government "National unity government").[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-315)[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-316) President [Ashraf Ghani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashraf_Ghani "Ashraf Ghani"), having fled the country during the Taliban advance to either [Tajikistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tajikistan "Tajikistan") or [Uzbekistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbekistan "Uzbekistan"), emerged in the United Arab Emirates and said that he supported such negotiations and was in talks to return to Afghanistan.[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-317)[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-318) Many figures within the Taliban generally agreed that continuation of the [2004 Constitution of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Constitution_of_Afghanistan "2004 Constitution of Afghanistan") may, if correctly applied, be workable as the basis for the new religious state as their objections to the former government were political, and not religious.[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-319)

Hours after the final flight of American troops left Kabul on 30 August, a Taliban official interviewed said that a new government would likely be announced as early as Friday 3 September after [Jumu'ah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumu%27ah "Jumu'ah"). It was added that [Hibatullah Akhundzada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hibatullah_Akhundzada "Hibatullah Akhundzada") would be officially named [Emir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_the_Islamic_Emirate_of_Afghanistan "Head of the Islamic Emirate of Afghanistan"), with cabinet ministers being revealed at the [Arg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arg_\(Kabul\) "Arg (Kabul)") in an official ceremony. Abdul Ghani Baradar would be named [head of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_government "Head of government") as [Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Afghanistan "Prime Minister of Afghanistan"), while other important positions would go to [Sirajuddin Haqqani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirajuddin_Haqqani "Sirajuddin Haqqani") and [Mullah Yaqoob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mullah_Yaqoob "Mullah Yaqoob"). Beneath the supreme leader, day-to-day governance will be entrusted to the [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Afghanistan#Islamic_Emirate_\(2021%E2%80%93present\) "Cabinet of Afghanistan").[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-320)

In a report by CNN-News18, sources said the new government was going to be governed similarly to Iran with Hibatullah Akhundzada as supreme leader similar to the role of [Saayid Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Khamenei "Ali Khamenei"), and would be based out of [Kandahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandahar "Kandahar"). Baradar or Yaqoob would be head of government as [Prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister "Prime minister"). The government's ministries and agencies will be under a cabinet presided over by the Prime Minister. The Supreme Leader would preside over an [executive body](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_\(government\) "Executive (government)") known as the Supreme Council with anywhere from 11 to 72 members. [Abdul Hakim Haqqani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hakim_Haqqani "Abdul Hakim Haqqani") is likely to be promoted to [Chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_justice "Chief justice"). According to the report, the new government will take place within the framework of an amended [1964 Constitution of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Constitution_of_Afghanistan "1964 Constitution of Afghanistan").[\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-321) Government formation was delayed due to concerns about forming a broad-based government acceptable to the international community.[\[315\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-322) It was later added however that the Taliban's Rahbari Shura, the group's leadership council was divided between the hardline Haqqani Network and moderate Abdul Ghani Baradar over appointments needed to form an "inclusive" government. Reports claimed that this culminated in a skirmish which led to Baradar being injured and treated in Pakistan, however this was denied by Baradar himself.[\[316\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-323)[\[317\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-324)

As of early September 2021 the Taliban were planning the cabinet to be men-only. Journalists and other human rights activists, mostly women, [protested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Afghan_protests "2021 Afghan protests") in [Herat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herat "Herat") and Kabul, calling for women to be included.[\[318\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-325) The [acting Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Afghanistan#Islamic_Emirate_\(2021%E2%80%93present\) "Cabinet of Afghanistan") announced on 7 September was men-only, and the [Ministry of Women's Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Women%27s_Affairs_\(Afghanistan\) "Ministry of Women's Affairs (Afghanistan)") was abolished.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-BBCHardliners-308)

As of June 2024, no country has recognized the [Taliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban "Taliban") government as the legitimate authorities of Afghanistan, with the U.N adding that recognition was impossible so long as restrictions on female education and employment remained.[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-309)[\[319\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-326) On 16 September 2024, the Taliban suspended polio vaccination campaigns in Afghanistan, as reported by the United Nations, posing a significant risk to global polio eradication efforts.[\[320\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-327)

### Administrative divisions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Afghanistan\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Administrative divisions")]

Main articles: [Provinces of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Afghanistan "Provinces of Afghanistan") and [Districts of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Afghanistan "Districts of Afghanistan")

Afghanistan is administratively divided into 34 provinces (*[wilayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilayat "Wilayat")*).[\[321\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-328) Each province has a governor and a capital. The country is further divided into nearly 400 provincial [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Afghanistan "Districts of Afghanistan"), each of which normally covers a city or several villages. Each district is represented by a district governor.

The [provincial governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_current_governors_of_Afghanistan "List of current governors of Afghanistan") are now appointed by the [Prime Minister of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Afghanistan "Prime Minister of Afghanistan"), and the district governors are selected by the provincial governors.[\[322\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-329) The provincial governors are representatives of the central government in Kabul and are responsible for all administrative and formal issues within their provinces. There are also provincial councils that are elected through direct and general elections for four years.[\[323\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-330) The functions of provincial councils are to take part in provincial development planning and to participate in the monitoring and appraisal of other provincial governance institutions.

According to article 140 of the constitution and the presidential decree on electoral law, mayors of cities should be elected through free and direct elections for a four-year term. In practice however, mayors are appointed by the government.[\[324\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-331)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Afghanistan_provinces_numbered.png/330px-Afghanistan_provinces_numbered.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Afghanistan_provinces_numbered.png)

Afghanistan is divided into [34 provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Afghanistan "Provinces of Afghanistan"), which are further divided into a [number of districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Afghanistan "Districts of Afghanistan").
